fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x00000203F4E33420>

kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226:

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x00000203F4CF7320>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

storage\connection.py:465: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x00000203F4CF7320>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x00000203F4E33420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x00000203F4CF7320>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

storage\connection.py:465: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001ABA4137320>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000001ABA4273420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001ABA4137320>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001ABA4137320>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000001ABA4273420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001ABA4137320>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x00000246379BE900>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001345E317260>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000001345E453420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001345E317260>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001345E317260>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

storage\connection.py:465: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001345E317260>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000001345E453420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:465: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000002AF8204E7E0>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000002AF82063420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000002AF8204E7E0>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001E8F1472870>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000001E8F1483420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226:

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001E8F1472870>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

            raise ValueError(f"Invalid privacy_level: '{privacy_level}'. Accepted: {sorted(VALID_PRIVACY_LEVELS)}")
    
        def _do(conn, u, fn, pw, pl, s):
            conn.execute(
                "INSERT INTO projects (uuid, folder_name, primary_wing, privacy_level, summary) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?)",
                (u, fn, pw, pl, s))
            return {"uuid": u, "folder_name": fn, "primary_wing": pw, "privacy_level": pl}

storage\connection.py:465: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001E8F1472870>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000001E8F1483420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001E8F1472870>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

storage\connection.py:465: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001BC218FF4A0>
fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000001BC21913420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001BC218FF4A0>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

fn = <function SqliteStore.create_project.<locals>._do at 0x000001BC21913420>
args = ('stress-test-project-uuid-static-12345', 'stress-test-project', 'financeiro', 'PUBLIC', 'Projeto de teste de stress')
kwargs = {}

    def submit(self, fn: Callable, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        """Queues a _WriteJob and blocks until execution completes or worker dies.
    
        Args:
            fn: Callable(conn, *args, **kwargs) to be executed on the writer thread.
            *args: Positional arguments passed to fn.
            **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to fn.
    
        Returns:
            The return value of fn.
    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the queue was not started, was stopped, or if the
                worker thread died during startup or execution.
            Any exception thrown by fn is re-raised in the calling thread.
        """
        if not self._started or self._stopped:
            raise RuntimeError("SerializedWriteQueue is not running. Call start() first.")
    
        if (
            self._startup_error is not None
            or self._fatal_error is not None
            or not self._thread.is_alive()
        ):
            raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        job = _WriteJob(fn=fn, args=args, kwargs=kwargs)
        self._queue.put(job)
    
        # Wait in short liveness-polling increments without imposing a total job timeout
        while not job.result_event.wait(timeout=POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS):
            if not self._thread.is_alive() and not job.result_event.is_set():
                raise self._build_worker_dead_error()
    
        if job.error is not None:
            logger.error("Job execution failed: %s", job.error)
>           raise job.error

storage\connection.py:226: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _

self = <storage.connection.SerializedWriteQueue object at 0x000001BC218FF4A0>

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        """Main loop of the writer thread.
    
        Opens the write connection with bounded startup retry on lock contention,
        restores regime PRAGMAs (WAL, busy_timeout=5000, foreign_keys=ON),
        consumes jobs until receiving sentinel (None), and drains pending jobs
        if an unexpected fatal error terminates the loop.
        """
        logger.debug("Worker thread (sqlite-writer) started. Connecting to database...")
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
    
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                conn = sqlite3.connect(
                    self._db_path, timeout=STARTUP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
                )
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    
                # Handshake PRAGMAs + restore regime busy_timeout=5000ms (Invariant 1)
                conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;")
                conn.execute(f"PRAGMA busy_timeout={REGIME_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};")
                conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")
                self._conn = conn
                break
            except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                msg_lower = str(exc).lower()
                is_lock_err = (
                    "database is locked" in msg_lower or "database is busy" in msg_lower
                )
                if is_lock_err and attempt < MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES:
                    backoff = min(
                        INITIAL_BACKOFF_SECONDS * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
                        MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS,
                    )
                    logger.warning(
                        "SerializedWriteQueue startup attempt %d/%d failed with '%s'. Retrying in %.3fs...",
                        attempt,
                        MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                        exc,
                        backoff,
                    )
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                    continue
    
                logger.error(
                    "SerializedWriteQueue startup failed on attempt %d/%d: %s",
                    attempt,
                    MAX_CONNECT_RETRIES,
                    exc,
                )
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
            except BaseException as exc:
                if conn is not None:
                    try:
                        conn.close()
                    except Exception as close_exc:
                        logger.debug("Error closing failed startup connection: %s", close_exc)
                    conn = None
    
                logger.error("SerializedWriteQueue unexpected startup failure: %s", exc)
                self._startup_error = exc
                self._running = False
                self._drain_pending_with_error(exc)
                self._ready.set()
                return
    
        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
    
                start_time = time.time()
                try:
>                   job.result = job.fn(self._conn, *job.args, **job.kwargs)
                                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

        logger.debug("Worker thread configured WAL and busy_timeout successfully.")
        self._ready.set()
    
        try:
            while self._running:
                try:
                    job = self._queue.get(timeout=0.5)
                except queue.Empty:
                    continue
    
                if job is None:
                    logger.debug("Worker thread received stop sentinel.")
                    break
